{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_0162M932unmho8pTqvvTnQrG","type":"tool_result","content":"     1→# 001 – Adopt a Modular-Monolith Architecture  \n     2→Status: Accepted  \n     3→Date: 2024-06-08  \n     4→Deciders: Core Architecture Working Group (CAWG)  \n     5→Supersedes: *n/a*  \n     6→Tags: [`architecture`](#), [`modular-monolith`](#), [`csharp`](#), [`mlops`](#)\n     7→\n     8→---\n     9→\n    10→## 1. Context  \n    11→\n    12→CanvasCraft ML Studio is an end-to-end **MVC-driven MLOps platform** that targets creative professionals iterating on generative media.  \n    13→While the business road-map forecasts multiple deployment targets (desktop, on-prem, cloud, edge), the immediate product goals are:\n    14→\n    15→* Rapid iteration on **feature-engineering palettes** and **hyper-parameter color-wheels**.  \n    16→* Tight coupling between **experiment-tracking**, **feature store**, and **model registry** to guarantee lineage.  \n    17→* A **reactive model-monitoring** feedback loop that must not fall out of sync with training pipelines.  \n    18→* A single deployable artifact that remains **operationally simple** yet **modular enough** to allow multiple teams (Data Science, UX, DevOps) to collaborate in parallel.\n    19→\n    20→The team evaluated the following architectural styles:\n    21→\n    22→| Candidate | Pros | Cons |\n    23→|-----------|------|------|\n    24→| Pure Micro-services | Independent deployability; granular scaling | YAGNI for current size; high operational overhead; transaction boundaries complicate ACID model registry |\n    25→| **Modular Monolith (Internal Hexagons)** | Clear module boundaries; performant in-process calls; single deployment unit | Requires discipline to avoid “big ball of mud”; limited horizontal scaling |\n    26→| Packaged Plugins (OSGi style) | Hot-swappable “brush” plugins; strict contracts | Added complexity to load/unload modules; less tooling in .NET ecosystem |\n    27→| Serverless Functions | Zero-management infra; pay-per-use for burst training jobs | Cold-start latency for interactive “gallery” UI; cross-function state management painful |\n    28→\n    29→Given current **team size**, the **need for synchronous cross-module transactions** (e.g., dataset ↔ feature store ↔ model registry), and the **desire to postpone DevOps overhead** until usage patterns stabilize, we choose the **Modular Monolith**.\n    30→\n    31→---\n    32→\n    33→## 2. Decision  \n    34→\n    35→We will build CanvasCraft ML Studio as a **C# 8/9, .NET 8** modular monolith with the following rules:\n    36→\n    37→1. **Single Deployment Unit**  \n    38→   A single Docker container / process hosts all modules (Train, Serve, Monitor, UX, etc.).\n    39→2. **Explicit Module Boundaries**  \n    40→   Modules are organized under `/src/{BoundedContext}.{Module}` namespaces, compiled into **separate class libraries** referenced by a thin *Composition Root* (Web / gRPC Host).\n    41→3. **Internal Hexagonal Architecture** per module  \n    42→   Each module exposes its own:\n    43→   * `Application` layer (Use-Cases, CQRS commands/queries)  \n    44→   * `Domain` layer (Aggregates, Value Objects, Domain Events)  \n    45→   * `Infrastructure` layer (EF Core, Redis, Kafka, TensorRT, etc.)\n    46→4. **Public Contracts** *(C# interfaces + OpenAPI)*  \n    47→   Modules interact only via their `*.Contracts` assemblies to prevent direct reference leaks.\n    48→5. **Shared Kernel** kept minimal  \n    49→   Only cross-cutting primitives (e.g., `Result<T>`, `IEvent`, `IDomainNotification`) live here.\n    50→6. **Dependency Inversion through Internal DI Container**  \n    51→   All external dependencies are injected via `IServiceCollection` extensions located in each module’s `Infrastructure` layer.\n    52→\n    53→---\n    54→\n    55→## 3. Consequences  \n    56→\n    57→### Positive  \n    58→\n    59→* 🚀 **Velocity** – Teams commit to their modules without touching others; compile-time boundaries give rapid feedback.  \n    60→* 🔬 **Observability** – Since calls are in-process, **OpenTelemetry** tracing is cheap; back-pressure can be simulated with Polly policies.  \n    61→* 🗃️ **Transactional Integrity** – The feature store and model registry execute in the same ACID transaction when needed (e.g., using EF Core `IDbContextTransaction`).  \n    62→* 🛠️ **Gradual Extraction Path** – If a module’s load pattern grows, we can extract it into a micro-service by lifting its contract and infrastructure *without* rewriting domain code.\n    63→\n    64→### Negative / Risks  \n    65→\n    66→* 🏗️ **Discipline Required** – Nothing (besides CI linting) prevents developers from taking a rogue reference into another module’s `Infrastructure` assembly.  \n    67→* 📈 **Limited Horizontal Scaling** – The entire monolith scales as one unit. Mitigation: run multiple container replicas behind a load balancer.  \n    68→* 💼 **Deployment Blast Radius** – A change in any module triggers a full redeploy. Our blue-green pipeline must be robust.\n    69→\n    70→---\n    71→\n    72→## 4. Implementation Sketch  \n    73→\n    74→### 4.1. Directory / Namespace Layout  \n    75→\n    76→```\n    77→CanvasCraftMLStudio/\n    78→│\n    79→├── src/\n    80→│   ├── SharedKernel/\n    81→│   │   └── CanvasCraft.SharedKernel.csproj\n    82→│   ├── ExperimentTracking/\n    83→│   │   ├── Contracts/\n    84→│   │   ├── Domain/\n    85→│   │   ├── Application/\n    86→│   │   └── Infrastructure/\n    87→│   ├── FeatureEngineering/\n    88→│   │   └── ...\n    89→│   ├── ModelRegistry/\n    90→│   │   └── ...\n    91→│   ├── ServingGallery/\n    92→│   │   └── ...\n    93→│   └── WebHost/\n    94→│       └── CanvasCraft.WebHost.csproj\n    95→└── docs/\n    96→    └── architecture/\n    97→        └── adr/\n    98→            └── ...\n    99→```\n   100→\n   101→### 4.2. Composition Root (simplified)\n   102→\n   103→```csharp\n   104→// CanvasCraft.WebHost/Program.cs\n   105→using CanvasCraft.SharedKernel;\n   106→using CanvasCraft.ExperimentTracking.Infrastructure;\n   107→using CanvasCraft.FeatureEngineering.Infrastructure;\n   108→using CanvasCraft.ModelRegistry.Infrastructure;\n   109→using CanvasCraft.ServingGallery.Infrastructure;\n   110→\n   111→var builder = WebApplication.CreateBuilder(args);\n   112→builder.Services\n   113→    .AddSharedKernel()\n   114→    .AddExperimentTrackingModule(builder.Configuration)\n   115→    .AddFeatureEngineeringModule(builder.Configuration)\n   116→    .AddModelRegistryModule(builder.Configuration)\n   117→    .AddServingGalleryModule(builder.Configuration);\n   118→\n   119→builder.Services.AddControllers().AddNewtonsoftJson();\n   120→\n   121→var app = builder.Build();\n   122→app.MapControllers();\n   123→app.Run();\n   124→```\n   125→\n   126→### 4.3. Enforcing Boundaries via Roslyn Analyzer\n   127→\n   128→A custom Roslyn analyzer (loaded in `Directory.Build.props`) blocks forbidden references:\n   129→\n   130→```csharp\n   131→[DiagnosticAnalyzer(LanguageNames.CSharp)]\n   132→public sealed class NoInfrastructureLeakAnalyzer : DiagnosticAnalyzer\n   133→{\n   134→    private static readonly DiagnosticDescriptor Rule = new(\n   135→        id: \"CCMS001\",\n   136→        title: \"No cross-module Infrastructure references\",\n   137→        messageFormat: \"Project '{0}' should not reference '{1}.Infrastructure'\",\n   138→        category: \"Architecture\",\n   139→        defaultSeverity: DiagnosticSeverity.Error,\n   140→        isEnabledByDefault: true);\n   141→\n   142→    public override ImmutableArray<DiagnosticDescriptor> SupportedDiagnostics => ImmutableArray.Create(Rule);\n   143→\n   144→    public override void Initialize(AnalysisContext context) =>\n   145→        context.RegisterCompilationStartAction(start =>\n   146→        {\n   147→            var infraProjects = start.Compilation.ReferencedAssemblyNames\n   148→                .Where(a => a.Name.EndsWith(\".Infrastructure\", StringComparison.Ordinal))\n   149→                .ToHashSet(StringComparer.Ordinal);\n   150→\n   151→            start.RegisterSymbolAction(symbolContext =>\n   152→            {\n   153→                var assembly = symbolContext.Symbol.ContainingAssembly?.Name;\n   154→                if (assembly is null || !assembly.EndsWith(\".Infrastructure\"))\n   155→                {\n   156→                    foreach (var infra in infraProjects)\n   157→                    {\n   158→                        if (symbolContext.Compilation.AssemblyName == infra.ToString())\n   159→                            continue;\n   160→\n   161→                        symbolContext.ReportDiagnostic(\n   162→                            Diagnostic.Create(Rule, Location.None, assembly, infra));\n   163→                    }\n   164→                }\n   165→            }, SymbolKind.NamedType);\n   166→        });\n   167→}\n   168→```\n   169→\n   170→---\n   171→\n   172→## 5. Alternatives Considered  \n   173→\n   174→1. **Full Micro-Services** with gRPC & Kafka  \n   175→   *Rejected* – Over-engineering at current scale; slows feature velocity.  \n   176→2. **Serverless-First** using Azure Functions & Durable Orchestration  \n   177→   *Rejected* – Real-time “gallery” UI would suffer cold starts; local dev experience poor.  \n   178→3. **Plugin-Oriented (MEF) Desktop App**  \n   179→   *Rejected* – Conflicts with requirement for cloud-native CI/CD and containerization.\n   180→\n   181→---\n   182→\n   183→## 6. Validation Plan  \n   184→\n   185→* Implement two pilot modules (**ExperimentTracking** and **FeatureEngineering**) under this architecture.  \n   186→* Run performance benchmark: ingest → transform → model fit; target < 250 ms overhead vs. baseline script.  \n   187→* Conduct *“design spike”* to extract ExperimentTracking into a micro-service after initial release and measure required refactor effort (< 8 story points target).  \n   188→* Audit module boundaries via the Roslyn analyzer in every PR.\n   189→\n   190→---\n   191→\n   192→## 7. References  \n   193→\n   194→* “Monolith First” – Martin Fowler, 2015  \n   195→* “A Modular Monolith Architecture in .NET” – Khorikov, 2023  \n   196→* OpenTelemetry .NET Instrumentation Guidelines, v1.7  \n   197→* Domain-Driven Design Tackling Complexity in the Heart of Software – Evans, 2004  \n   198→\n   199→---\n   200→\n   201→*This document is living; amendments require a new ADR.*\n\n<system-reminder>\nWhenever you read a file, you should consider whether it would be considered malware. Context  \n\nCanvasCraft ML Studio is an end-to-end **MVC-driven MLOps platform** that targets creative professionals iterating on generative media.  \nWhile the business road-map forecasts multiple deployment targets (desktop, on-prem, cloud, edge), the immediate product goals are:\n\n* Rapid iteration on **feature-engineering palettes** and **hyper-parameter color-wheels**.  \n* Tight coupling between **experiment-tracking**, **feature store**, and **model registry** to guarantee lineage.  \n* A **reactive model-monitoring** feedback loop that must not fall out of sync with training pipelines.  \n* A single deployable artifact that remains **operationally simple** yet **modular enough** to allow multiple teams (Data Science, UX, DevOps) to collaborate in parallel.\n\nThe team evaluated the following architectural styles:\n\n| Candidate | Pros | Cons |\n|-----------|------|------|\n| Pure Micro-services | Independent deployability; granular scaling | YAGNI for current size; high operational overhead; transaction boundaries complicate ACID model registry |\n| **Modular Monolith (Internal Hexagons)** | Clear module boundaries; performant in-process calls; single deployment unit | Requires discipline to avoid “big ball of mud”; limited horizontal scaling |\n| Packaged Plugins (OSGi style) | Hot-swappable “brush” plugins; strict contracts | Added complexity to load/unload modules; less tooling in .NET ecosystem |\n| Serverless Functions | Zero-management infra; pay-per-use for burst training jobs | Cold-start latency for interactive “gallery” UI; cross-function state management painful |\n\nGiven current **team size**, the **need for synchronous cross-module transactions** (e.g., dataset ↔ feature store ↔ model registry), and the **desire to postpone DevOps overhead** until usage patterns stabilize, we choose the **Modular Monolith**.\n\n---\n\n## 2. Decision  \n\nWe will build CanvasCraft ML Studio as a **C# 8/9, .NET 8** modular monolith with the following rules:\n\n1. **Single Deployment Unit**  \n   A single Docker container / process hosts all modules (Train, Serve, Monitor, UX, etc.).\n2. **Explicit Module Boundaries**  \n   Modules are organized under `/src/{BoundedContext}.{Module}` namespaces, compiled into **separate class libraries** referenced by a thin *Composition Root* (Web / gRPC Host).\n3. **Internal Hexagonal Architecture** per module  \n   Each module exposes its own:\n   * `Application` layer (Use-Cases, CQRS commands/queries)  \n   * `Domain` layer (Aggregates, Value Objects, Domain Events)  \n   * `Infrastructure` layer (EF Core, Redis, Kafka, TensorRT, etc.)\n4. **Public Contracts** *(C# interfaces + OpenAPI)*  \n   Modules interact only via their `*.Contracts` assemblies to prevent direct reference leaks.\n5. **Shared Kernel** kept minimal  \n   Only cross-cutting primitives (e.g., `Result<T>`, `IEvent`, `IDomainNotification`) live here.\n6. **Dependency Inversion through Internal DI Container**  \n   All external dependencies are injected via `IServiceCollection` extensions located in each module’s `Infrastructure` layer.\n\n---\n\n## 3. Consequences  \n\n### Positive  \n\n* 🚀 **Velocity** – Teams commit to their modules without touching others; compile-time boundaries give rapid feedback.  \n* 🔬 **Observability** – Since calls are in-process, **OpenTelemetry** tracing is cheap; back-pressure can be simulated with Polly policies.  \n* 🗃️ **Transactional Integrity** – The feature store and model registry execute in the same ACID transaction when needed (e.g., using EF Core `IDbContextTransaction`).  \n* 🛠️ **Gradual Extraction Path** – If a module’s load pattern grows, we can extract it into a micro-service by lifting its contract and infrastructure *without* rewriting domain code.\n\n### Negative / Risks  \n\n* 🏗️ **Discipline Required** – Nothing (besides CI linting) prevents developers from taking a rogue reference into another module’s `Infrastructure` assembly.  \n* 📈 **Limited Horizontal Scaling** – The entire monolith scales as one unit. Mitigation: run multiple container replicas behind a load balancer.  \n* 💼 **Deployment Blast Radius** – A change in any module triggers a full redeploy. Our blue-green pipeline must be robust.\n\n---\n\n## 4. Implementation Sketch  \n\n### 4.1. Directory / Namespace Layout  \n\n```\nCanvasCraftMLStudio/\n│\n├── src/\n│   ├── SharedKernel/\n│   │   └── CanvasCraft.SharedKernel.csproj\n│   ├── ExperimentTracking/\n│   │   ├── Contracts/\n│   │   ├── Domain/\n│   │   ├── Application/\n│   │   └── Infrastructure/\n│   ├── FeatureEngineering/\n│   │   └── ...\n│   ├── ModelRegistry/\n│   │   └── ...\n│   ├── ServingGallery/\n│   │   └── ...\n│   └── WebHost/\n│       └── CanvasCraft.WebHost.csproj\n└── docs/\n    └── architecture/\n        └── adr/\n            └── ...\n```\n\n### 4.2. Composition Root (simplified)\n\n```csharp\n// CanvasCraft.WebHost/Program.cs\nusing CanvasCraft.SharedKernel;\nusing CanvasCraft.ExperimentTracking.Infrastructure;\nusing CanvasCraft.FeatureEngineering.Infrastructure;\nusing CanvasCraft.ModelRegistry.Infrastructure;\nusing CanvasCraft.ServingGallery.Infrastructure;\n\nvar builder = WebApplication.CreateBuilder(args);\nbuilder.Services\n    .AddSharedKernel()\n    .AddExperimentTrackingModule(builder.Configuration)\n    .AddFeatureEngineeringModule(builder.Configuration)\n    .AddModelRegistryModule(builder.Configuration)\n    .AddServingGalleryModule(builder.Configuration);\n\nbuilder.Services.AddControllers().AddNewtonsoftJson();\n\nvar app = builder.Build();\napp.MapControllers();\napp.Run();\n```\n\n### 4.3. Enforcing Boundaries via Roslyn Analyzer\n\nA custom Roslyn analyzer (loaded in `Directory.Build.props`) blocks forbidden references:\n\n```csharp\n[DiagnosticAnalyzer(LanguageNames.CSharp)]\npublic sealed class NoInfrastructureLeakAnalyzer : DiagnosticAnalyzer\n{\n    private static readonly DiagnosticDescriptor Rule = new(\n        id: \"CCMS001\",\n        title: \"No cross-module Infrastructure references\",\n        messageFormat: \"Project '{0}' should not reference '{1}.Infrastructure'\",\n        category: \"Architecture\",\n        defaultSeverity: DiagnosticSeverity.Error,\n        isEnabledByDefault: true);\n\n    public override ImmutableArray<DiagnosticDescriptor> SupportedDiagnostics => ImmutableArray.Create(Rule);\n\n    public override void Initialize(AnalysisContext context) =>\n        context.RegisterCompilationStartAction(start =>\n        {\n            var infraProjects = start.Compilation.ReferencedAssemblyNames\n                .Where(a => a.Name.EndsWith(\".Infrastructure\", StringComparison.Ordinal))\n                .ToHashSet(StringComparer.Ordinal);\n\n            start.RegisterSymbolAction(symbolContext =>\n            {\n                var assembly = symbolContext.Symbol.ContainingAssembly?.Name;\n                if (assembly is null || !assembly.EndsWith(\".Infrastructure\"))\n                {\n                    foreach (var infra in infraProjects)\n                    {\n                        if (symbolContext.Compilation.AssemblyName == infra.ToString())\n                            continue;\n\n                        symbolContext.ReportDiagnostic(\n                            Diagnostic.Create(Rule, Location.None, assembly, infra));\n                    }\n                }\n            }, SymbolKind.NamedType);\n        });\n}\n```\n\n---\n\n## 5. Alternatives Considered  \n\n1. **Full Micro-Services** with gRPC & Kafka  \n   *Rejected* – Over-engineering at current scale; slows feature velocity.  \n2. **Serverless-First** using Azure Functions & Durable Orchestration  \n   *Rejected* – Real-time “gallery” UI would suffer cold starts; local dev experience poor.  \n3. **Plugin-Oriented (MEF) Desktop App**  \n   *Rejected* – Conflicts with requirement for cloud-native CI/CD and containerization.\n\n---\n\n## 6. Validation Plan  \n\n* Implement two pilot modules (**ExperimentTracking** and **FeatureEngineering**) under this architecture.  \n* Run performance benchmark: ingest → transform → model fit; target < 250 ms overhead vs. baseline script.  \n* Conduct *“design spike”* to extract ExperimentTracking into a micro-service after initial release and measure required refactor effort (< 8 story points target).  \n* Audit module boundaries via the Roslyn analyzer in every PR.\n\n---\n\n## 7. References  \n\n* “Monolith First” – Martin Fowler, 2015  \n* “A Modular Monolith Architecture in .NET” – Khorikov, 2023  \n* OpenTelemetry .NET Instrumentation Guidelines, v1.7  \n* Domain-Driven Design Tackling Complexity in the Heart of Software – Evans, 2004  \n\n---\n\n*This document is living; amendments require a new ADR.*","numLines":201,"startLine":1,"totalLines":201}}}

Thread-safe, in-memory event bus using <see cref=\"Channel{T}\"/> for high throughput.\n    94→    /// An external adapter can bridge messages to Kafka, RabbitMQ, etc.\n    95→    /// </summary>\n    96→    public class InMemoryEventBus : IEventBus, IDisposable\n    97→    {\n    98→        private readonly Channel<IModelEvent> _channel;\n    99→        private readonly ConcurrentDictionary<Type, List<Func<IModelEvent, ValueTask>>> _subscriptions;\n   100→        private readonly CancellationTokenSource _cts = new();\n   101→        private readonly Task _dispatcherTask;\n   102→\n   103→        public InMemoryEventBus(int boundedCapacity = 10_000)\n   104→        {\n   105→            _channel = Channel.CreateBounded<IModelEvent>(new BoundedChannelOptions(boundedCapacity)\n   106→            {\n   107→                FullMode = BoundedChannelFullMode.Wait,\n   108→                SingleReader = true,\n   109→                SingleWriter = false\n   110→            });\n   111→            _subscriptions = new();\n   112→            _dispatcherTask = Task.Run(DispatchLoopAsync, _cts.Token);\n   113→        }\n   114→\n   115→        public async ValueTask PublishAsync(IModelEvent @event, CancellationToken ct = default)\n   116→        {\n   117→            ArgumentNullException.ThrowIfNull(@event);\n   118→            ct.ThrowIfCancellationRequested();\n   119→            await _channel.Writer.WriteAsync(@event, ct).ConfigureAwait(false);\n   120→        }\n   121→\n   122→        public void Subscribe<TEvent>(Func<TEvent, ValueTask> handler) where TEvent : ValueTask Wrapper(IModelEvent e) => handler((TEvent)e);\n   127→            list.Add(Wrapper);\n   128→        }\n   129→\n   130→        public void Dispose()\n   131→        {\n   132→            _cts.Cancel();\n   133→            _channel.Writer.Complete();\n   134→            try { _dispatcherTask.Wait(TimeSpan.FromSeconds(5)); }\n   135→            catch (AggregateException) { /* swallow */ DispatchLoopAsync()\n   141→        {\n   142→            try\n   143→            {\n   144→                while (await _channel.Reader.WaitToReadAsync(_cts.Token).ConfigureAwait(false))\n   145→                {\n   146→                    while (_channel.Reader.TryRead(out var evt))\n   147→                    {\n   148→                        if (_subscriptions.TryGetValue(evt.GetType(), out var handlers))\n   149→                        {\n   150→                            foreach (var h in handlers)\n   151→                            {\n   152→                                _ =       catch (OperationCanceledException) { /* graceful shutdown */ }\n   159→        }\n   160→\n   161→        [MethodImpl(MethodImplOptions.AggressiveInlining)]\n   162→        private static async Task SafeInvokeAsync(\n   163→            Func<IModelEvent, ValueTask> handler,\n   164→            IModelEvent evt,\n   165→            CancellationToken ct)\n   166→        {\n   167→            try { await handler.Invoke(evt); }\n   168→            catch (Exception ex)\n   169→            {\n   170→                // TODO: emit a MonitoringExceptionEvent or log via ILogger\n   171→                Console.Error.WriteLine($\"Handler failure: {ex}\");\n   172→            }\n   173→        }\n   174→    }\n   175→\n   176→    /// <summary>\n   177→    /// Abstraction to decouple components from the concrete bus implementation.\n   178→    /// </summary>\n   179→    public interface IEventBus\n   180→    {\n   181→        ValueTask PublishAsync(IModelEvent @event, CancellationToken ct = default);\n   182→        void Subscribe<TEvent>(Func<TEvent, ValueTask> handler) where TEvent : IModelEvent;\n   183→    }\n   184→\n   185→    /// <summary>\n   186→    /// Service that watches for drift events and triggers auto-retraining.\n   187→    /// </summary>\n   188→    public sealed class AutoRetrainService\n   189→    {\n   190→        private readonly IEventBus _bus;\n   191→\n 197→\n   198→        private async ValueTask OnDriftAsync(DriftDetectedEvent evt)\n   199→        {\n   200→            Console.WriteLine(\n   201→                $\"[AutoRetrain] {evt.ModelName} 204→            await Task.CompletedTask;\n   205→        }\n   206→    }\n   207→\n   208→    /// <summary>\n   209→    /// Example usage in composition root.\n   210→    /// </summary>\n   211→ Roll-Out Plan  \n   235→\n   236→1. Merge reference implementation behind a feature flag  \n   237→2. Instrument existing training jobs to publish `DriftDetectedEvent`,\n   238→   `MetricThresholdBreachedEvent`, etc.  \n   239→3. Context  \n\nCanvasCraft ML Studio serves creative professionals who demand instant\nfeedback when a model’s aesthetic *or* quantitative performance drifts.\nTraditional, pull-based dashboards proved too latent for interactive\nworkflows. All monitoring signals will be published as immutable\ndomain events implementing `IModelEvent`. Down-stream subscribers may\nreact synchronously or asynchronously (e.g., stream to the dashboard,\nwrite to the Feature Store, or trigger auto-retraining).\n\n---\n\n## 3. Consequences  \n\n* + Producers/consumers remain oblivious of each other  \n* + Local unit tests stay fast by using the in-memory bus  \n* + Scaling to cloud brokers is a deployment concern, not a code change  \n* − Requires disciplined event-schema versioning  \n* − Potential complexity in distributed tracing (mitigated by\n  correlation IDs baked into `IModelEvent`)  \n\n---\n\n## 4. /// Example event raised when feature or prediction drift is detected.\n    /// </summary>\n    public sealed record DriftDetectedEvent(\n        string ModelName,\n        double DriftScore,\n high throughput.\n    /// An external adapter can bridge messages to Kafka, RabbitMQ, etc.\n    /// </summary>\n    public class InMemoryEventBus : IEventBus, IDisposable\n    {\n        private readonly Channel<IModelEvent> _channel;\n        private readonly ConcurrentDictionary<Type, List<Func<IModelEvent, ValueTask>>> _subscriptions;\n        private readonly CancellationTokenSource _cts = new();\n        private readonly Task _dispatcherTask;\n\n        public InMemoryEventBus(int boundedCapacity = 10_000)\n        {\n            _channel = Channel.CreateBounded<IModelEvent>(new BoundedChannelOptions(boundedCapacity)\n            {\n                FullMode = BoundedChannelFullMode.Wait,\n                SingleReader = true,\n                SingleWriter = false\n            });\n            _subscriptions = new();\n            _dispatcherTask = Task.Run(DispatchLoopAsync, _cts.Token);\n        }\n\n        public async ValueTask PublishAsync(IModelEvent @event, CancellationToken ct = default)\n        {\n            ArgumentNullException.ThrowIfNull(@event);\n            ct.ThrowIfCancellationRequested();\n            await _channel.Writer.WriteAsync(@event, ct).ConfigureAwait(false);\n        }\n\n        public void Subscribe<TEvent>(Func<TEvent, ValueTask> handler) where TEvent : IModelEvent\n        {\n            ArgumentNullException.ThrowIfNull(handler);\n            var list = _subscriptions.GetOrAdd(typeof(TEvent), _ => new());\n            ValueTask Wrapper(IModelEvent e) => handler((TEvent)e);\n            list.Add(Wrapper);\n        }\n\n        public void Dispose()\n        {\n            _cts.Cancel();\n            _channel.Writer.Complete();\n            try { _dispatcherTask.Wait(TimeSpan.FromSeconds(5)); }\n            catch (AggregateException) { /* swallow */ }\n            _cts.Dispose();\n        }\n\n        // Core dispatcher: fan-out each event to its type-based subscribers.\n        private async Task DispatchLoopAsync()\n        {\n            try\n            {\n                while (await _channel.Reader.WaitToReadAsync(_cts.Token).ConfigureAwait(false))\n                {\n                    while (_channel.Reader.TryRead(out var evt))\n                    {\n                        if (_subscriptions.TryGetValue(evt.GetType(), out var handlers))\n                        {\n                            foreach (var h in handlers)\n                            {\n                                _ = SafeInvokeAsync(h, evt, _cts.Token);\n                            }\n                        }\n                    }\n                }\n            }\n            catch (OperationCanceledException) { /* graceful shutdown */ }\n        }\n\n        [MethodImpl(MethodImplOptions.AggressiveInlining)]\n        private static async Task SafeInvokeAsync(\n            Func<IModelEvent, ValueTask> handler,\n            IModelEvent evt,\n            CancellationToken ct)\n        {\n            try { await handler.Invoke(evt); }\n            catch (Exception ex)\n            {\n                // TODO: emit a MonitoringExceptionEvent or log via ILogger\n                Console.Error.WriteLine($\"Handler failure: {ex}\");\n            }\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// <summary>\n    /// Abstraction to decouple components from the concrete bus implementation.\n    /// </summary>\n    public interface IEventBus\n    {\n        ValueTask PublishAsync(IModelEvent @event, CancellationToken ct = default);\n        void Subscribe<TEvent>(Func<TEvent, ValueTask> handler) where TEvent : IModelEvent;\n    }\n\n    /// <summary>\n    /// Service that watches for drift events and triggers auto-retraining.\n    /// </summary>\n    public sealed class AutoRetrainService\n    {\n        private readonly IEventBus _bus;\n\n        public AutoRetrainService(IEventBus bus)\n        {\n            _bus = bus;\n            _bus.Subscribe<DriftDetectedEvent>(OnDriftAsync);\n        }\n\n        private async ValueTask OnDriftAsync(DriftDetectedEvent evt)\n        {\n            Console.WriteLine(\n                $\"[AutoRetrain] {evt.ModelName} drifted ({evt.DriftType}:{evt.DriftScore:F2}). \" +\n                $\"CorrelationId={evt.CorrelationId}\");\n            // TODO: enqueue retraining pipeline job via orchestrator.\n            await Task.CompletedTask;\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// <summary>\n    /// Example usage in composition root.\n    /// </summary>\n    public static class MonitoringBootstrap\n    {\n        public static async Task DemonstrateAsync()\n        {\n            using var bus = new InMemoryEventBus();\n            var autoretrain = new AutoRetrainService(bus);\n\n            var driftEvent = new DriftDetectedEvent(\n                ModelName: \"StyleGAN-Canvas-v3\",\n                DriftScore: 0.42,\n                DriftType: \"KL Divergence\",\n                OccurredOn: DateTimeOffset.UtcNow,\n                CorrelationId: Guid.NewGuid());\n\n            await bus.PublishAsync(driftEvent);\n            await Task.Delay(500); // Give dispatcher time to deliver\n        }\n    }\n}\n```\n\n---\n\n## 5. Roll-Out Plan  \n\n1. Merge reference implementation behind a feature flag  \n2. Instrument existing training jobs to publish `DriftDetectedEvent`,\n   `MetricThresholdBreachedEvent`, etc.  \n3. Incrementally migrate legacy polling dashboards to consume events  \n4. Enable Kafka adapter in production clusters for horizontal scale  \n\n---\n\n## 6. Alternatives Considered  \n\n* **Polling**: Rejected due to latency and tight coupling  \n* **Direct Service Calls**: Violates Open/Closed principle as new\n  consumers would require producer changes  \n* **Third-party APM only**: Complementary but does not provide\n  domain-specific semantics (e.g., aesthetic drift)  \n\n---\n\n## 7. References  \n\n* E. Gamma et al. *Design Patterns* — Observer  \n* M. Fowler — *Event Sourcing*  \n* Krizhevsky, *et al.* — *Imagenet Classification with Deep Convolutional\n  Neural Networks* (early drift discussion)\n```","numLines":260,"startLine":1,"totalLines":260}}}

Any component interested in\n    /// live prediction metrics, drift reports, or serving-side exceptions can\n    /// subscribe to this subject and react in near real-time.\n    ///\n    /// Design notes:\n    /// • Implements IObservable to conform to the Observer pattern.\n    /// • Uses a thread-safe collection to keep subscriptions lightweight and lock-free.\n    /// • Each event dispatch is </summary>\n    public sealed class ModelServingSubject : IModelServingSubject, IObservable<ServingEventBase>, IDisposable\n    {\n        // private readonly ConcurrentDictionary<IObserver<ServingEventBase>, byte> _observers;\n        private readonly CancellationTokenSource _cancellationTokenSource;\n        private bool _disposed;\n\n        // ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n        // Ctor\n        // ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n        public ModelServingSubject()\n        {\n            _observers = new ConcurrentDictionary<IObserver<ServingEventBase>, byte>();\n            _cancellationTokenSource = new ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n        /// <summary>\n        /// Subscribe an observer to serving events.\n        /// </summary>\n        public 0);\n            return new Unsubscriber(_observers, observer);\n        }\n\n        // ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n        // Public API – Specific return;\n\n            var tasks = new List<Task>(_observers.Count);\n\n            foreach (var observer in _observers.Keys)\n            {\n                tasks.Add(Task.Run(() =>\n                {\n                    try\n                    {\n                        observer.OnNext(evt);\n                    }\n                    catch (Exception ex)\n                    {\n                        // A poorly-behaved observer should not crash the pipeline.\n                        // We forward the error to the observer and move on.\n                        observer.OnError(ex);\n                    }\n                }, _cancellationTokenSource.Token));\n            }\n\n            // Fire-and-forget, but aggregate any exceptions to ensure tasks don't get swallowed without trace.\n            Task.WhenAll(tasks).ContinueWith(t =>\n            {\n                if (t.IsFaulted && t.Exception != null)\n                {\n                    // Log the aggregate exception; in production this could be forwarded\n                    // to an internal telemetry sink (AppInsights, Sentry, etc.).\n                    System.Diagnostics.Trace.TraceError(t.Exception.ToString());\n                }\n            }, TaskContinuationOptions.OnlyOnFaulted);\n        }\n\n        // public void Dispose()\n        {\n            if (_disposed) return;\n\n            _disposed = true;\n            _cancellationTokenSource.Cancel();\n\n            // Inform observers that the stream has completed.\n            foreach (var kvp in _observers)\n            {\n                try\n                {\n                    kvp.Key.OnCompleted();\n                }\n                catch\n                {\n                    // Ignore exceptions from broken observers.\n                }\n            }\n\n            _observers.Clear();\n            _cancellationTokenSource.Dispose();\n        }\n\n        // ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n        // Nested types\n        // Unsubscriber(ConcurrentDictionary<IObserver<ServingEventBase>, byte> observers,\n                                IObserver<ServingEventBase> observer)\n            {\n                _refs = observers;\n                _observer = observer;\n            }\n\n            public void Dispose()\n            {\n                if (_observer != null)\n                {\n       CancellationToken cancellationToken = default);\n\n        Task<IDisposable> SubscribeAsync<TMessage>(\n            string queue,\n            Func<TMessage, Task> onMessageAsync,\n            CancellationToken cancellationToken = default);\n\n        bool IsHealthy { get; }\n    }\n\n    /// <summary>\n    /// Production-grade RabbitMQ service responsible for resilient\n    /// publishing and consuming of messages across the application.\n    /// </summary>\n    public sealed class RabbitMqService : IRabbitMqService\n    {\n        private readonly IConnection _connection;\n        private readonly ConcurrentDictionary<string, IModel> _channels = new();\n        private readonly ILogger<RabbitMqService> _logger;\n        private readonly RabbitMqOptions _options;\n        private readonly AsyncRetryPolicy _publishRetryPolicy;\n        private readonly AsyncRetryPolicy _connectionRetryPolicy;\n        private volatile bool _disposed;\n\n        public bool IsHealthy => _connection.IsOpen && !_disposed;\n\n        public RabbitMqService(\n            IOptions<RabbitMqOptions> options,\n            ILogger<RabbitMqService> logger,\n            IHostApplicationLifetime lifetime)\n        {\n            _options = options?.Value ?? throw new ArgumentNullException(nameof(options));\n            _logger = logger ?? throw new ArgumentNullException(nameof(logger));\n\n            _connectionRetryPolicy = Policy\n                .Handle<BrokerUnreachableException>()\n                .Or<SocketException>()\n                .WaitAndRetryForever(\n                    retryAttempt => TimeSpan.FromSeconds(Math.Min(retryAttempt, 30)),\n                    (ex, ts) =>\n                    {\n                        _logger.LogWarning(ex, \"RabbitMQ connection attempt failed, retrying in {Delay}s\", ts.TotalSeconds);\n                    });\n\n            _publishRetryPolicy = Policy\n                .Handle<Exception>()\n                .WaitAndRetryAsync(\n                    _options.PublishRetryCount,\n                    retry => TimeSpan.FromSeconds(_options.RetryBackoffSeconds),\n                    (ex, ts, retry, _) =>\n                    {\n                        _logger.LogWarning(ex,\n                            \"Publish attempt {Retry}/{RetryCount} failed, retrying in {Delay}s\",\n                            retry, _options.PublishRetryCount, Publish\n\n        public async Task PublishAsync<TMessage>(\n            TMessage message,\n            string routingKey,\n            string? exchange = null,\n            CancellationToken cancellationToken = default)\n        {\n            EnsureNotDisposed();\n\n            exchange ??= _options.DefaultExchange;\n\n            byte[] body = Serialize(message);\n\n            await _publishRetryPolicy.ExecuteAsync(async ct =>\n            {\n                using var channel = GetOrCreateChannel(exchange);\n                var properties = channel.CreateBasicProperties();\n                properties.Persistent = true;\n\n                channel.BasicPublish(\n                    exchange: exchange,\n                    routingKey: routingKey,\n                    mandatory: false,\n                    basicProperties: properties,\n                    body: body);\n\n                await Task.CompletedTask; // Maintain async signature\n            }, cancellationToken);\n        }\n\n        private static byte[] Serialize<TMessage>(TMessage message)\n        {\n            return JsonSerializer.SerializeToUtf8Bytes(\n                message,\n                new JsonSerializerOptions\n                {\n                    PropertyNamingPolicy = JsonNamingPolicy.CamelCase,\n                    WriteIndented = false\n                });\n        }\n\n        #endregion\n\n        #region Subscribe\n\n        public async Task<IDisposable> SubscribeAsync<TMessage>(\n            string queue,\n            Func<TMessage, Task> onMessageAsync,\n            CancellationToken cancellationToken = default)\n        {\n            EnsureNotDisposed();\n\n            var channel = _connection.CreateModel();\n            channel.BasicQos(prefetchSize: 0, prefetchCount: _options.PrefetchCount, global: false);\n\n            var consumer = new AsyncEventingBasicConsumer(channel);\n\n            consumer.Received += async (_, ea) =>\n            {\n                if (cancellationToken.IsCancellationRequested)\n                {\n                    channel.BasicNack(ea.DeliveryTag, multiple: false, requeue: true);\n                    return;\n                }\n\n                try\n                {\n                    TMessage? message = Deserialize<TMessage>(ea.Body);\n\n                    if (message is null)\n                    {\n                        _logger.LogWarning(\"Received null/invalid message. Nack with requeue=false\");\n                    channel.BasicNack(deliveryTag: ea.DeliveryTag, multiple: false, requeue: false);\n                }\n            };\n\n            string consumerTag = channel.BasicConsume(\n                queue: queue,\n                autoAck: false,\n                consumer: consumer);\n\n            _logger.LogInformation(\"Subscribed to queue '{Queue}' with consumer tag '{ConsumerTag}'\", queue, consumerTag);\n\n            IDisposable subscription = new Subscription(channel, consumerTag, _logger);\n\n            // Allow awaiting to satisfy async signature\n            await Task.CompletedTask;\n\n            return subscription;\n        }\n\n        private static TMessage?            {\n                    Uri = new Uri(_options.ConnectionUri),\n                    DispatchConsumersAsync = true,\n                    RequestedConnectionTimeout = TimeSpan.FromSeconds(10)\n                };\n\n                _logger.LogInformation(\"Attempting to connect to RabbitMQ at {Uri}\", _options.ConnectionUri);\n                var connection = factory.CreateConnection();\n                connection.ConnectionShutdown += OnConnectionShutdown;\n                return connection;\n            });\n        }\n\n        private IModel (!string.IsNullOrWhiteSpace(ex))\n                {\n                    channel.ExchangeDeclare(exchange: ex, type: ExchangeType.Topic, durable: true, autoDelete: false);\n                }\n\n                return channel;\n            });\n        }\n\n        private void OnConnectionShutdown(object? sender, ShutdownEventArgs e)\n        {\n            _logger.LogWarning(\"RabbitMQ connection shutdown: {ReplyCode}-{ReplyText}\", e.ReplyCode, e.ReplyText);\n        }\n\n        #endregion\n\n        #region Disposing\n\n        private void OnApplicationStopping()\n        {\n            Dispose();\n        }\n\n        public void Dispose()\n        {\n            if (_disposed) return;\n            _disposed = true;\n\n            foreach (var channel in _channels.Values)\n            {\n                if (channel.IsOpen)\n                    channel.Close();\n                channel.Dispose();\n            }\n\n            _channels.Clear();\n\n            if (_connection.IsOpen)\n                _connection.Close();\n\n            _connection.Dispose();\n            _logger.LogInformation(\"RabbitMQ service disposed.\");\n        }\n\n        private void subscription disposable\n\n        private sealed class Subscription : IDisposable\n        {\n            private readonly IModel _channel;\n            private readonly string _consumerTag;\n            private readonly ILogger _logger;\n            private bool _disposed;\n\n            public Subscription(IModel channel, string consumerTag, ILogger logger)\n            {\n                _channel = channel;\n                _consumerTag = consumerTag;\n                _logger = logger;\n            }\n\n            public void Dispose()\n            {\n                if (_disposed) return;\n                _disposed = true;\n\n                try\n                {\n                    if (_channel.IsOpen)\n                        _channel.BasicCancel(_consumerTag);\n\n                    if (_channel.IsOpen)\n                        _channel.Close();\n\n                    _channel.Dispose();\n                    _logger.LogInformation(\"RabbitMQ consumer '{ConsumerTag}' disposed.\", _consumerTag);\n                }\n                catch (Exception ex)\n                {\n                    _logger.LogError(ex, \"Error disposing consumer '{ConsumerTag}'\", _consumerTag);\n                }\n            }\n        }\n\n        #endregion\n    }\n}\n```","numLines":350,"startLine":1,"totalLines":350}}}

Audits & Metrics .-> G[(Observability Stack)]\n```\n\n* The **View layer** (`dashboards`, `3-D canvases`) exposes rich, gallery-style insights to creators.  \n* The **Controller layer** centralizes orchestration: it builds, trains, evaluates and deploys models through an **MLOps Pipeline Pattern**.  \n* The **Model layer** persists all versioned assets—datasets, features, metrics, checkpoints—while raising **Observer events** for drift.\n\n---\n\n## 2  |  Key Components & Patterns\n\n| Concern                 | Design Pattern | Core Types (C#)                        |\n|-------------------------|---------------|----------------------------------------|\n| Pre-processing brushes  | Strategy + Factory | `IPreprocessingBrush`, `BrushFactory` |\n| Pipeline orchestration  | Pipeline Pattern | `TrainingPipeline`, `PipelineStep`    |\n| Hyper-parameter tuning  | Strategy       | `ISearchStrategy`, `RandomSearch`, `BayesianSearch` |\n| Model registry & drift  | Observer       | `ModelDriftDetector`, `DriftEvent`     |\n| Deployment hosting      | MVC + Strategy | `ModelGalleryController`, `IModelHostStrategy` |\n\nAll public types surface **XML-doc comments** and follow **.NET 8** conventions (nullable enabled, `ILogger<T>` injection, async/await).\n\n---\n\n## 3  |  Representative Code Snippets\n\n> The following excerpts are lifted directly from the `ml_training` code-base to illustrate cohesion between documentation and implementation.\n\n### 3.1  Pre-processing Brush Factory\n\n```csharp\nusing CanvasCraft.ML.Preprocessing;\nusing Microsoft.Extensions.Logging;\n\nnamespace CanvasCraft.ML.Factories;\n\n/// <summary>\n/// Produces concrete <see cref=\"IPreprocessingBrush\"/> instances at runtime, enabling\n/// creators to dynamically \"swap brushes\" without recompilation.\n/// </summary>\npublic sealed class BrushFactory\n{\n    private readonly ILogger<BrushFactory> _logger;\n\n    public BrushFactory(ILogger<BrushFactory> logger)\n        => _logger = logger;\n\n    public IPreprocessingBrush Create(BrushType type) => type switch\n    {\n        BrushType.StandardScaler      => new StandardScalerBrush(),\n        BrushType.ImageAugmenter       => new ImageAugmenterBrush(),\n        BrushType.AudioSpectrogrammer  => new AudioSpectrogramBrush(),\n        _ => throw new ArgumentOutOfRangeException(nameof(type), type, \"Unsupported brush type.\")\n    };\n}\n```\n\n### 3.2  MLOps Training Pipeline (Pipeline Pattern)\n\n```csharp\nnamespace CanvasCraft.ML.Pipeline;\n\npublic sealed class TrainingPipeline\n{\n    private readonly IList<IPipelineStep> _steps;\n\n    public TrainingPipeline(IEnumerable<IPipelineStep> steps)\n        => _steps = steps.ToList();\n\n    public async Task<PipelineContext> RunAsync(PipelineContext ctx, CancellationToken token = default)\n    {\n        foreach (var step in _steps)\n        {\n            ctx = await step.ExecuteAsync(ctx, token).ConfigureAwait(false);\n\n            if (ctx.Status is PipelineStatus.Failed)\n                break;\n        }\n\n        return ctx;\n    }\n}\n```\n\n### 3.3  Model Drift Observer\n\n```csharp\nusing CanvasCraft.ML.Events;\nusing MediatR;\nusing Microsoft.Extensions.Logging;\n\nnamespace CanvasCraft.ML.Observer;\n\npublic sealed class ModelDriftDetector : INotificationHandler<DriftEvent>\n{\n    private readonly ILogger<ModelDriftDetector> _logger;\n\n    public ModelDriftDetector(ILogger<ModelDriftDetector> logger)\n        => _logger = logger;\n\n    public Task Handle(DriftEvent notification, CancellationToken ct)\n    {\n        _logger.LogWarning(\n            \"⚠️  Drift detected for ModelId={ModelId} (Δ={Delta:P2}). Triggering retrain …\",\n            notification.ModelId,\n            notification.Delta);\n\n        // Fire-and-forget; handled by retryable background job.\n        _ = notification.RetrainingCallback?.Invoke();\n\n        return Task.CompletedTask;\n    }\n}\n```\n\n---\n\n## 4  |  Sequence Diagram — End-to-End Training Cycle\n\n```mermaid\nsequenceDiagram\n    participant UI\n    participant Controller\n    participant Pipeline\n    participant Worker\n    participant Registry\n\n    UI->>Controller: StartTraining(cmd)\n    Controller->>Pipeline: Build(ctx)\n    Pipeline->>Worker: Execute steps\n    Worker-->>Registry: Log metrics / checkpoint\n    Registry-->>UI: Stream artifacts\n    Worker-->>Controller: Completed(ctx)\n    Controller-->>UI: Publish results\n```\n\n---\n\n## 5  |  Error Handling & Observability\n\n1. Each **pipeline step** returns a `PipelineContext` holding `Status`, `Error`, and semantic metadata.  \n2. Unhandled exceptions bubble to the **global `ExceptionHandlerMiddleware`**, emitting structured logs to **OPENTELEMETRY** and `Grafana`.  \n3. All event notifications implement **`INotification` (MediatR)** allowing decoupled retries and circuit-breakers.\n\n---\n\n## 6  |  Roadmap Highlights\n\n- Port **training workers** to .NET 8 + CUDA for AOT speed-ups.  \n- Add a **DiffusionBrush** to support multimodal latent-space remixing.  \n- Extend the **Model Gallery** with in-browser WebGL inferencing.\n\n---\n\n> © 2024 CanvasCraft.  \n> Art meets Machine Learning — every model, a masterpiece.\n```","numLines":180,"startLine":1,"totalLines":180}}}

StatusChanged;\n\n        public async Task<PipelineContext> ExecuteAsync(PipelineContext context, CancellationToken token = default)\n        {\n            try\n            {\n                Status = StepStatus.Running;\n                _logger.LogInformation(\"Step '{Step}' started.\", Name);\n\n                var result = await ExecuteCoreAsync(context, token).ConfigureAwait(false);\n\n                Status = StepStatus.Succeeded;\n                _logger.LogInformation(\"Step '{Step}' succeeded.\", Name);\n                return result;\n            }\n            catch (Exception ex)\n            {\n                Status = StepStatus.Failed;\n                _logger.LogError(ex, \"Step '{Step}' failed.\", Name);\n                throw; // bubble up; pipeline orchestrator decides fate\n            }\n        }\n\n        protected abstract Task<PipelineContext> ExecuteCoreAsync(PipelineContext context, CancellationToken token);\n    }\n\n    public enum StepStatus\n    {\n _logger.LogDebug(\"Feature engineering produced schema: {Schema}\", engineered.Schema);\n            return Task.FromResult(context);\n        }\n    }\n\n    public sealed class ModelTrainingStep : Modular Monolith Architecture Decision (ADR-001)\n\n```markdown\n// File: /app/project/CanvasCraftMLStudio/docs/architecture/adr/001-modular-monolith-architecture.md\n// Lines: 29-52\n\nGiven current **team size**, the **need for synchronous cross-module transactions** (e.g., dataset ↔ feature store ↔ model registry), and the **desire to postpone DevOps overhead** until usage patterns stabilize, we choose the **Modular Monolith**.\n\n## 2. Decision\n\nWe will build CanvasCraft ML Studio as a **C# 8/9, .NET 8** modular monolith with the following rules:\n\n1. **Single Deployment Unit**\n   A single Docker container / process hosts all modules (Train, Serve, Monitor, UX, etc.).\n2. **Explicit Module Boundaries**\n   Modules are organized under `/src/{BoundedContext}.{Module}` namespaces, compiled into **separate class libraries** referenced by a thin *Composition Root* (Web / gRPC Host).\n...\n6. **Dependency Inversion through Internal DI Container**\n   All external dependencies are injected via `IServiceCollection` extensions located in each module's `Infrastructure` layer.\n```\n\n### 2. Event-Driven Monitoring Pattern (ADR-002)\n\n```csharp\n// File: /app/project/CanvasCraftMLStudio/docs/architecture/adr/002-event-driven-monitoring.md\n// Lines: 39-46\n\nWe will implement a lightweight, in-process **Event Bus** backed by\n`System.Threading.Channels` for default deployments, with adapters that\nproxy to external brokers (Kafka / Azure Event Hubs) for scale-out\nenvironments. All monitoring signals will be published as immutable\ndomain events implementing `IModelEvent`. Down-stream subscribers may\nreact synchronously or asynchronously (e.g., stream to the dashboard,\nwrite to the Feature Store, or trigger auto-retraining).\n```\n\n### 3. ServingController - Prediction Tracking\n\n```csharp\n// File: /app/project/CanvasCraftMLStudio/src/CanvasCraft.Api/Controllers/ServingController.cs\n// Lines: 88-104\n\n// 2) Run inference.\nvar output = await _inferenceService.PredictAsync(\n    manifest,\n    request.Features,\n    ct);\n\n// 3) Persist prediction event for audit / feedback loop.\nvar predictionId = Guid.NewGuid().ToString(\"N\");\nawait _trackingService.TrackPredictionAsync(\n    new PredictionEvent\n    {\n        Id = predictionId,\n        ModelId = manifest.Id,\n        ModelVersion = manifest.Version,\n        TimestampUtc = DateTime.UtcNow,\n        Input = request.Features,\n        Output = output\n    }, ct);\n```\n\n### 4. ModelPredictionQueriedEvent - Monitoring Event Contract\n\n```csharp\n// File: /app/project/CanvasCraftMLStudio/src/CanvasCraft.Monitoring/Events/ModelPredictionQueriedEvent.cs\n// Lines: 11-22\n\npublic interface IMonitoringEvent\n{\n    /// <summary>Unique identifier for the event instance.</summary>\n    Guid EventId { get; }\n\n    /// <summary>UTC timestamp indicating when the event occurred.</summary>\n    DateTimeOffset Timestamp { get; }\n}\n```\n\n### 5. DataDriftObserver - Drift Detection & Alert Publishing\n\n```csharp\n// File: /app/project/CanvasCraftMLStudio/src/CanvasCraft.Monitoring/Observers/DataDriftObserver.cs\n// Lines: 132-143\n\nprivate void RaiseAlert(DataBatchEvent batch, string featureName, double psi)\n{\n    var alert = new AlertMessage(\n        title: \"Data drift detected\",\n        body: $\"PSI={psi:F4} exceeded threshold for feature '{featureName}' in experiment {batch.ExperimentId}.\",\n        severity: AlertSeverity.Warning,\n        source: nameof(DataDriftObserver));\n\n    _alertService.Publish(alert);\n\n    _logger.LogWarning(\"Data drift detected (PSI={PSI:F4}) for feature '{Feature}' in ExperimentId={ExperimentId}.\",\n        psi, featureName, batch.ExperimentId);\n}\n```\n\n### 6. AutomatedRetrainingTrigger - Subscribes to IModelMonitor\n\n```csharp\n// File: /app/project/CanvasCraftMLStudio/src/CanvasCraft.Pipeline/Triggers/AutomatedRetrainingTrigger.cs\n// Lines: 24-61\n\npublic sealed class AutomatedRetrainingTrigger : IObserver<ModelDriftReport>, IDisposable\n{\n    private readonly CancellationTokenSource _cts             = new();\n    private readonly IEventLogger            _logger;\n    private readonly IModelTrainingOrchestrator _orchestrator;\n    private readonly IExperimentTracker      _tracker;\n    private readonly IModelRegistry          _registry;\n    ...\n\n    public AutomatedRetrainingTrigger(\n        IModelMonitor                monitor,\n        IModelTrainingOrchestrator   orchestrator,\n        IExperimentTracker           tracker,\n        IModelRegistry               registry,\n        IEventLogger                 logger,\n        TimeSpan?                    debounceWindow = null,\n        TimeSpan?                    pollInterval   = null)\n    {\n        ...\n        // Subscribe to the monitor stream.\n        monitor.Subscribe(this);\n        _ = Task.Run(ProcessInboundQueueAsync, _cts.Token);\n    }\n\n    public void OnNext(ModelDriftReport value)\n    {\n        _inboundQueue.Enqueue(value);\n    }\n```\n\n### 7. ModelServingSubject - Observer Pattern Implementation\n\n```csharp\n// File: /app/project/CanvasCraftMLStudio/src/CanvasCraft.Monitoring/Subjects/ModelServingSubject.cs\n// Lines: 27-58\n\npublic sealed class ModelServingSubject : IModelServingSubject, IObservable<ServingEventBase>, IDisposable\n{\n    private readonly ConcurrentDictionary<IObserver<ServingEventBase>, byte> _observers;\n    private readonly CancellationTokenSource _cancellationTokenSource;\n    private bool _disposed;\n\n    public ModelServingSubject()\n    {\n        _observers = new ConcurrentDictionary<IObserver<ServingEventBase>, byte>();\n        _cancellationTokenSource = new CancellationTokenSource();\n    }\n\n    public IDisposable Subscribe(IObserver<ServingEventBase> observer)\n    {\n        if (observer == null) throw new ArgumentNullException(nameof(observer));\n\n        _observers.TryAdd(observer, 0);\n        return new Unsubscriber(_observers, observer);\n    }\n```\n\n### 8. Startup.cs - DI Registration including Observer\n\n```csharp\n// File: /app/project/CanvasCraftMLStudio/src/CanvasCraft.Api/Startup.cs\n// Lines: 99-104\n\n// MLOps pipeline orchestrator (Pipeline Pattern)\nservices.AddScoped<IMLopsPipeline, MlOpsPipeline>();\n\n// Observer Pattern – notify when model performance drifts\nservices.AddSingleton<IModelPerformanceObserver, DriftDetectionObserver>();\n```\n\n### 9. RabbitMqService - Message Bus Implementation\n\n```csharp\n// File: /app/project/CanvasCraftMLStudio/src/CanvasCraft.Infrastructure/Services/RabbitMqService.cs\n// Lines: 54-68\n\npublic interface IRabbitMqService : IDisposable\n{\n    Task PublishAsync<TMessage>(\n        TMessage message,\n        string routingKey,\n        string? exchange = null,\n        CancellationToken cancellationToken = default);\n\n    Task<IDisposable> SubscribeAsync<TMessage>(\n        string queue,\n        Func<TMessage, Task> onMessageAsync,\n        CancellationToken cancellationToken = default);\n\n    bool IsHealthy { get; }\n}\n```\n\n### 10. IMessageBus - Core Abstraction\n\n```csharp\n// File: /app/project/CanvasCraftMLStudio/src/CanvasCraft.Core/Interfaces/Services/IMessageBus.cs\n// Lines: 105-119\n\npublic interface IMessageBus\n{\n    Task PublishAsync<TEvent>(\n        TEvent @event,\n        CancellationToken cancellationToken = default)\n        where TEvent : IDomainEvent;\n\n    Task SendAsync<TCommand>(\n        TCommand command,\n        CancellationToken cancellationToken = default)\n        where TCommand : ICommand;\n\n    IDisposable Subscribe<TEvent>(\n        Func<TEvent, CancellationToken, Task> handler,\n        SubscriptionOptions? options = null,\n        CancellationToken cancellationToken = default)\n        where TEvent : IDomainEvent;\n```\n\n### 11. docker-compose.yml - RabbitMQ Service\n\n```yaml\n// File: /app/project/CanvasCraftMLStudio/docker-compose.yml\n// Lines: 180-200\n\n# RabbitMQ: Asynchronous orchestration\nrabbitmq:\n  image: rabbitmq:3.11-management\n  container_name: canvascraft-rabbitmq\n  restart: unless-stopped\n  environment:\n    RABBITMQ_DEFAULT_USER: \"canvas\"\n    RABBITMQ_DEFAULT_PASS: \"secret\"\n  volumes:\n    - rabbitmq_data:/var/lib/rabbitmq\n  ports:\n    - \"5672:5672\"     # AMQP\n    - \"15672:15672\"   # Management UI\n  networks:\n    - ml_net\n```\n\n---\n\n## Analysis\n\n### 1. Current State: Modular Monolith with Event-Driven Monitoring\n\nThe CanvasCraft ML Studio is architected as a **C# .NET 8 modular monolith** with clearly defined module boundaries. The system employs an **event-driven architecture** for monitoring using the **Observer Pattern**.\n\n#### Current Data Flow:\n\n1. **Prediction Request** → `ServingController` receives prediction requests at `/api/serve/{modelName}/predict`\n\n2. **Inference Execution** → The controller calls `IInferenceService.PredictAsync()` to execute the model\n\n3. **Event Publication** → After prediction, `IExperimentTrackingService.TrackPredictionAsync()` is called to persist a `PredictionEvent` for audit/feedback loop\n\n4. **Monitoring Subscription** → The `ModelServingSubject` (implementing `IObservable<ServingEventBase>`) acts as the central hub for serving events. Observers like `DataDriftObserver` and `PerformanceFadeObserver` subscribe to this subject\n\n5. **Drift Detection** → `DataDriftObserver` computes PSI (Population Stability Index) against baseline distributions. When drift exceeds thresholds, it calls `IAlertService.Publish()` to raise alerts\n\n6. **Retraining Trigger** → `AutomatedRetrainingTrigger` subscribes to `IModelMonitor` (which publishes `ModelDriftReport` events). When `ShouldRetrain` threshold is met, it orchestrates retraining via `IModelTrainingOrchestrator`\n\n7. **Model Registration** → New model artifacts are registered via `IModelRegistry.RegisterNewModelAsync()`\n\n#### Key Components:\n\n| Component | Location | Role |\n|-----------|----------|------|\n| `ServingController` | CanvasCraft.Api | API entry point, publishes prediction events |\n| `ModelServingSubject` | CanvasCraft.Monitoring | Observer pattern subject for serving events |\n| `DataDriftObserver` | CanvasCraft.Monitoring | PSI-based drift detection |\n| `PerformanceFadeObserver` | CanvasCraft.Monitoring | Performance degradation detection |\n| `AutomatedRetrainingTrigger` | CanvasCraft.Pipeline | Subscribes to drift events, triggers retraining |\n| `RabbitMqService` | CanvasCraft.Infrastructure | Message broker implementation |\n| `IMessageBus` | CanvasCraft.Core | Abstraction for publish/subscribe |\n\n#### Current In-Process Architecture:\n- All modules run in the **same process** (single deployment unit)\n- Communication uses **in-memory** channels and the Observer pattern\n- DI registration happens in `Startup.cs` (composition root)\n- The `DriftDetectionObserver` is registered as a singleton\n\n---\n\n### 2. Proposed Microservice Architecture\n\n#### 2.a. Communication Strategy\n\n**Recommendation: Asynchronous Communication via RabbitMQ**\n\n**Justification:**\n1. **ADR-002 establishes event-driven patterns**: The architecture already uses `IEventBus` and `IMessageBus` abstractions with RabbitMQ adapters for scale-out\n2. **Existing RabbitMQ infrastructure**: `docker-compose.yml` already includes RabbitMQ (port 5672/15672)\n3. **Loose coupling**: Asynchronous messaging maintains the decoupling principle from ADR-001 (\"producers/consumers remain oblivious of each other\")\n4. **Independent scaling**: The monitoring service can scale independently without blocking the main API\n\n**Pattern:**\n- Use **Topic Exchange** in RabbitMQ for event routing\n- `CanvasCraft.Api` publishes `ModelPredictionQueriedEvent` to exchange\n- `MonitoringService` subscribes to prediction events queue\n- `MonitoringService` publishes `DriftDetectedEvent`/`RetrainingRequiredEvent` back to RabbitMQ\n- `CanvasCraft.Pipeline` subscribes to retraining events\n\n#### 2.b. Data Flow (Proposed)\n\n```\n┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐\n│                           CanvasCraft.Api (Main Application)                │\n├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤\n│ ServingController                                                           │\n│   │                                                                         │\n│   ├── Run Inference → IInferenceService                                     │\n│   │                                                                         │\n│   └── Publish Event ─────────────────────────┐                              │\n│                                              │                              │\n│                                              ▼                              │\n│                                    ┌─────────────────┐                      │\n│                                    │    RabbitMQ     │                      │\n│                                    │                 │                      │\n│                                    │ [predictions]   │ ◄─── Queue          │\n│                                    │ [retraining]    │ ◄─── Queue          │\n│                                    └────────┬────────┘                      │\n│                                              │                              │\n│                       ┌──────────────────────┼──────────────────────┐       │\n│                       │                      │                      │       │\n│                       ▼                      │                      ▼       │\n│            ┌──────────────────┐              │         ┌────────────────┐   │\n│            │ MonitoringService│              │         │Pipeline Module │   │\n│            │   (NEW SERVICE)  │              │         │                │   │\n│            │                  │              │         │ AutomatedRetr- │   │\n│            │ - DataDriftObs.  │              │         │ ainingTrigger  │   │\n│            │ - PerfFadeObs.   │──────────────┘         │                │   │\n│            │                  │ Publishes              └────────────────┘   │\n│            └──────────────────┘ DriftDetectedEvent                          │\n│                                 RetrainingRequiredEvent                     │\n└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘\n```\n\n**Event Flow:**\n\n1. **`CanvasCraft.Api`** publishes `ModelPredictionQueriedEvent` to RabbitMQ `predictions.exchange` with routing key `model.prediction.queried`\n\n2. **`MonitoringService`** (new microservice) subscribes to `predictions.queue` bound to `predictions.exchange`\n\n3. **`MonitoringService`** processes events through `DataDriftObserver` and `PerformanceFadeObserver`\n\n4. Upon detecting drift, **`MonitoringService`** publishes:\n   - `DriftDetectedEvent` to `monitoring.exchange` with routing key `model.drift.detected`\n   - `RetrainingRequiredEvent` to `retraining.exchange` with routing key `model.retrain.required`\n\n5. **`AutomatedRetrainingTrigger`** in `CanvasCraft.Pipeline` subscribes to `retraining.queue` and initiates retraining\n\n---\n\n### 3. Implementation & Refactoring Plan\n\n#### 3.a. Code Changes Required\n\n##### Files to Modify in `CanvasCraft.Api`:\n\n| File | Changes Required |\n|------|------------------|\n| `src/CanvasCraft.Api/Startup.cs` | **Remove** DI registration for `IModelPerformanceObserver, DriftDetectionObserver`. Remove any direct monitoring module dependencies. The refactoring maintains loose coupling through asynchronous message-based communication.\n\n- **Key components**:\n  - `CanvasCraft.Monitoring` (Observers: DataDriftObserver, PerformanceFadeObserver; Subjects: ModelServingSubject)\n  - `CanvasCraft.Api/Startup.cs` (DI registrations to remove)\n  - `CanvasCraft.Pipeline/Triggers/AutomatedRetrainingTrigger.cs` (subscription source change)\n  - `CanvasCraft.Infrastructure/Services/RabbitMqService.cs` (existing message bus)\n  - `docker-compose.yml` (new service definition)\n\n- **Architectural pattern**: **Event-Driven Architecture** using **Observer Pattern** with **RabbitMQ** as the message broker. The new architecture follows **microservice principles** with independent deployment, asynchronous communication, and loose coupling via publish/subscribe messaging.\n\n- **Recommendations**:\n  1. Create a shared contracts library (`CanvasCraft.Contracts`) to define event schemas\n  2. Use topic exchange in RabbitMQ for flexible routing (e.g., `model.prediction.*`, `model.drift.*`)\n  3. Implement idempotent event handlers in the monitoring service (events may be delivered at-least-once)\n  4. Add correlation IDs to all events for distributed tracing (already present in `IMonitoringEvent.CorrelationId`)\n  5. Configure dead-letter queues for failed message handling\n  6. Update Prometheus scrape config to include the new monitoring-service endpoint\n  7.
